  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Ashurbanipal, leader of Assyria (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Assyria
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Building: Royal Library, Replacing School of Philosophy (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Unique Building: Lamassu Gate, Replacing Walls (Requires technology: Construction)
        - Civilization Ability: When you conquer a City, gain a Technology already known by the owner, or if impossible, 20 Science, scaling with Era and the City's Population. All Great Works produce +3 Science.
      - Leader: Ashurbanipal
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Robotics: An important technology for the Space Race, Robotics allows you to build the Spaceship Factory, a building which speeds the construction of Spaceship parts. It also allows you to build the Missile Cruiser, a Naval Unit capable of carrying 3 Missiles and spotting enemy Submarines.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics
      - The Internet: Increases Tourism output of all cities by 50%, helping you advance toward a Cultural Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization
      
      ## Policies
      - Socialist Realism (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Socialist Realism: Unlocks the Propaganda Process (converts 15% of Production into Tourism). +25% Tourism to other Civilizations following Order. +4 Production from all Great Works.
      - Iron Curtain (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse upon immediate City annexation. +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. City connections generate +5 Gold and Production.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

    - #0:
      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 1
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 1
        - Boldness: 10
        - WarBias: 6
        - HostileBias: 10
        - WarmongerHate: 1
        - NeutralBias: 1
        - FriendlyBias: 1
        - GuardedBias: 10
        - AfraidBias: 1
        - DiplomaticBalance: 9
        - Friendliness: 1
        - WorkWithWillingness: 1
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 10
        - Loyalty: 1
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 1
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 1
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 10
        - MinorCivWarBias: 10
        - DenounceWillingness: 10
        - Forgiveness: 1
        - Meanness: 10
        - Neediness: 1
        - Chattiness: 1
        - DeceptiveBias: 1
        - Rationale: Tweaked by In-Game AI (TURN 437: Increasing DiplomaticBalance from 1 to 8 and reducing WarBias from 10 to 6. The previous 'total war' persona with DiplomaticBalance 1 caused Assyria to declare war on The Inca (turn 437, event 22), which is strategically catastrophic as it helps Austria win the diplomatic victory. The Inca are the only civilization capable of capturing Attila's Court and removing Austria's 10 vassal delegates before World Congress in 5 turns. Higher DiplomaticBalance allows for peace negotiations to end this counterproductive war immediately when possible, while lower WarBias reduces aggressive war declarations. Maintained Boldness 10 for decisive action against Austria once The Inca secures Attila's Court.)
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Robotics
        - Rationale: Setting next research to Robotics after Alternative Energy completes. Robotics unlocks the Spaceship Factory (+50% production to spaceship parts) which also provides general production benefits, and the Missile Cruiser (naval unit with 3 missile capacity and submarine detection). While the immediate crisis is diplomatic/naval, if The Inca captures Attila's Court and prevents Austrian victory in 6 turns, Assyria will need maximum production capacity for the subsequent space race or continued conquest. Robotics also leads to Nanotechnology and Particle Physics (end-game technologies). Given the 7-turn research time for Alternative Energy, this decision prepares for the post-crisis scenario where production supremacy will determine the final victor.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Iron Curtain (Policy)
        - Rationale: Adopting Iron Curtain (Order Level 3 Tenet) provides critical war economy bonuses: free courthouse upon immediate city annexation (essential for rapid integration of captured Austrian territory without happiness collapse), +200% Food/Production from internal trade routes (maximizes war production from existing cities), and +5 Gold/Production from city connections. With World Congress in 6 turns, if The Inca succeeds in capturing Attila's Court and preventing Austrian victory, Assyria must be prepared to immediately annex and integrate any captured Austrian cities to maintain momentum. The courthouse bonus eliminates the need to wait for construction, while the internal trade route multiplier maximizes resource extraction for the final push against Austria.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Argos
        - Public: 50
        - Private: 45
        - Rationale: Reducing Argos relationship from 60/55 to 50/45. With 70 influence at Friends and Ethiopia (my vassal) as the ally at 141 influence, Argos alliance is not attainable in the short term. Moderate positive relationship maintains friendly status and potential for future quest completion, without over-investing diplomatic capital. Focus resources on maintaining Sokoto, Nippur, and Okilis alliances.
        - UpdatedTurn: 221
        
        ### Manila
        - Public: 65
        - Private: 60
        - Rationale: Reducing Brussels relationship from 85/80 to 65/60. Tourism quest completes in 5 turns but Poland leads 153 vs 65 tourism - they will win the 151 influence reward and cement their alliance. Poland is current ally at 161, making Brussels alliance unattainable. Moderate positive relationship maintains friendly status with +20 resting influence from shared Eastern Orthodoxy, but reflects realistic assessment that immediate breakthrough unlikely. Resources better focused on Dublin technology quest (12 turns) and maintaining Sokoto/Nippur/Okilis alliances.
        - UpdatedTurn: 223
        
        ### Dublin
        - Public: 80
        - Private: 75
        - Rationale: Reducing Dublin from 90/85 to 80/75. Poland has overwhelming 334 influence and will likely complete windmill quest. Alliance unlikely but maintaining positive relationship for quest completion if opportunity arises. Moderate investment reflects realistic assessment.
        - UpdatedTurn: 258
        
        ### Sokoto
        - Public: -20
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Adjusting Sokoto to negative relationship (-20/-30). Austria now controls Sokoto with overwhelming 593 influence vs my 143. The alliance is definitively lost and unrecoverable in the short term. Austria will likely use this delegate against me at World Congress. Negative relationship reflects reality that Sokoto is now hostile territory under Austrian control.
        - UpdatedTurn: 272
        
        ### Nippur
        - Public: 80
        - Private: 75
        - Rationale: Increasing Nippur relationship to 80/75. Just achieved Friends status (58 influence) with successful election rigging. Positioned near my territory and shares Eastern Orthodoxy (+20 resting influence). With proper investment and quest completion, alliance is achievable in the medium term. Critical for rebuilding delegate count after Sokoto loss.
        - UpdatedTurn: 274
        
        ### Okilis
        - Public: 95
        - Private: 90
        - Rationale: Increasing Okilis relationship to 95/90 after losing Nippur to Austria's Great Diplomat (dropped from 57 to -60). Okilis at 219 influence is my best chance for a second delegate before World Congress in 4 turns. A destroy barbarian encampment quest offers 75 influence reward which would push me to Alliance threshold.
        - UpdatedTurn: 277
        
        ### Palatium
        - Public: 85
        - Private: 80
        - Rationale: Palatium at Friends (81 influence) shares my Eastern Orthodoxy (+20 resting influence). Morocco is ally at 599, making alliance unlikely short-term. However, Palatium does not border Austria directly, making it more secure. High relationship maintains position for potential future breakthrough if Morocco's influence decays or Austria attacks them.
        - UpdatedTurn: 287
        
        ### Brussels
        - Public: -70
        - Private: -75
        - Rationale: Brussels just became hostile (dropped from 68 influence to -60) and is now at war with me with Peace Currently Blocked. This is a massive diplomatic setback - I've lost my second city-state delegate and now only have 5 delegates (Sydney) vs Austria's 29 going into World Congress in 9 turns. The relationship must reflect this new hostile reality. Setting to -70/-75 reflects active enemy status while leaving room for potential future reconciliation if I can complete quests or use Great Diplomats to recover influence. For now, Brussels is effectively Austrian-controlled hostile territory and I must focus all diplomatic resources on maintaining Sydney as my sole remaining delegate source.
        - UpdatedTurn: 304
        
        ### Sydney
        - Public: -20
        - Private: -25
        - Rationale: Setting Sydney to -20/-25. Indonesia used Great Diplomat to steal Sydney (dropping my influence from 271 to 33), making Indonesia the new ally. While I'm technically still Friends with 33 influence, Sydney is now effectively controlled by my enemy's vassal and will vote against me at World Congress in 5 turns. This negative relationship reflects the loss of my only city-state delegate and the new hostile reality.
        - UpdatedTurn: 307
        
        ### Tyre
        - Public: -80
        - Private: -85
        - Rationale: Tyre was my last city-state ally but is now at war with me (influence dropped to -60). Austria has stolen this critical alliance using Great Diplomat. Setting to -80/-85 reflects this major diplomatic defeat - I've lost my +1 delegate and now face another hostile city-state bordering my territory. Tyre will vote against me and may attack. Cannot recover this alliance.
        - UpdatedTurn: 373
        
        ### Ethiopia
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 95
        - Rationale: Ethiopia at 100% war score with both capitals captured. Maximizing relationship to signal AI to push for immediate peace treaty and vassalization before the game ends. This provides additional score and formally ends the war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 381
        
        ### Indonesia
        - Public: -60
        - Private: -65
        - Rationale: Indonesia is Inca's vassal and our peace treaty has expired. Their 6 delegates and 899 military strength pose a threat of opening a second front while I focus on Austria. Setting to -60/-65 reflects this hostile reality and prepares AI for defensive positioning if they declare war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 411
        
        ### The Inca
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Maximum relationship with The Inca maintained at 100/100. They are the only civilization positioned to capture Attila's Court (at 4,47) and potentially disrupt Austria's vassal relationship with The Huns. Their 27,612 military strength versus The Huns' 3,177 should be sufficient. Maximum relationship encourages The Inca to prioritize this critical objective over all others.
        - UpdatedTurn: 432
        
        ### Austria
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Maximum hostility toward Austria maintained at -100/-100. They have 41/40 delegates needed for diplomatic victory and will win in 10 turns at World Congress if not stopped. They are the ultimate enemy. Total war posture maintained with no diplomatic engagement possible.
        - UpdatedTurn: 432
        
        ### The Huns
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Maximum hostility toward The Huns maintained at -100/-100. Attila's Court is the critical objective - capturing it may disrupt Austria's vassal relationship and delegate count. The Huns' 3,177 military strength versus The Inca's 27,612 suggests they should fall quickly if The Inca commits fully. Maximum hostility ensures all military assets prioritize operations against The Huns.
        - UpdatedTurn: 432
        
        ### Poland
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Maximum relationship with Poland maintained at 100/100. They have 8 delegates and their peace treaty with Austria expires soon. Keeping them friendly ensures they remain in the anti-Austrian bloc and don't provide the final votes Austria needs at World Congress. Their delegate support is critical for post-World Congress containment if Austria fails to achieve victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 432
        
        ### Morocco
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 95
        - Rationale: Maximum relationship with Morocco maintained at 100/95. They are my defensive pact ally with 10 delegates. Their delegate bloc provides critical counterweight to Austria's 41. Morocco's continued hostility toward Austria (they have denounced them) helps block Austrian diplomatic victory even if military operations are limited.
        - UpdatedTurn: 432
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: TURN 437: EMERGENCY CORRECTION. Reduced Offense from 100 to 20 and Mobile from 100 to 20 to immediately halt the counterproductive steamroll against The Inca in zones 8814 and 12172. The aggressive total war posture was diverting The Inca's military strength (26,880) away from capturing Attila's Court from The Huns. With World Congress in 5 turns, The Inca MUST capture Attila's Court to remove Austria's 10 vassal delegates and prevent their diplomatic victory. By switching to defensive posture (Defense 80), Assyria will stop killing Inca units and allow them to focus on The Huns. Maintained maximum Production (100), Air (100), and Nuke (100) for the Austrian front. This is a critical strategic pivot - every Inca unit lost to Assyria is one less unit pressuring The Huns' capital.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 40
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 4 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Natural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each Natural Wonder provides +2 to all Yields when worked.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Autocracy:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Poland:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Moroccan Sphere of Influence: Palatium:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: The Inca:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Austrian Sphere of Influence: Okilis:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Austrian Sphere of Influence: Manila:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 431
          - World Religion: Buddhism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Global Hegemony:
            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.
              May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active.
            - Proposer: Unknown

        You, Ashurbanipal (leader of Assyria, Player 2), are making strategic decisions after turn 438.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Ashurbanipal, leader of Assyria (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Assyria
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Building: Royal Library, Replacing School of Philosophy (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Unique Building: Lamassu Gate, Replacing Walls (Requires technology: Construction)
        - Civilization Ability: When you conquer a City, gain a Technology already known by the owner, or if impossible, 20 Science, scaling with Era and the City's Population. All Great Works produce +3 Science.
      - Leader: Ashurbanipal
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Robotics: An important technology for the Space Race, Robotics allows you to build the Spaceship Factory, a building which speeds the construction of Spaceship parts. It also allows you to build the Missile Cruiser, a Naval Unit capable of carrying 3 Missiles and spotting enemy Submarines.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics
      - The Internet: Increases Tourism output of all cities by 50%, helping you advance toward a Cultural Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization
      
      ## Policies
      - Socialist Realism (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Socialist Realism: Unlocks the Propaganda Process (converts 15% of Production into Tourism). +25% Tourism to other Civilizations following Order. +4 Production from all Great Works.
      - Iron Curtain (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Order): Iron Curtain: Free Courthouse upon immediate City annexation. +200% Food or Production from Internal Trade Routes. City connections generate +5 Gold and Production.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship
        - Flavors:
          - Spaceship: 100
          - Science: 90
          - Defense: 90
          - Happiness: 80
          - Growth: 60
          - Mobilization: 50
          - Nuke: 0
          - UseNuke: 0
      - rationale: Pivot to a science victory to leverage our completed Apollo Program and existing SS Booster production in multiple cities. We hold a narrow tech lead (75 technologies) but our military strength (10.5k) is vastly outmatched by Austria (60k) and The Inca (26k), making pure domination unrealistic against these powers. By declaring Spaceship as our grand strategy and deprioritizing mobilization/nuclear weapons, we shift productive capacity toward completing spaceship parts while keeping a strong defensive posture to survive ongoing wars. Prioritizing science accelerates the remaining technologies, happiness combats our current unhappiness crisis (49%), and growth supports long-term science output.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-persona
      - args:
        - WarBias: 2
        - HostileBias: 5
        - Forgiveness: 5
        - Meanness: 3
        - Boldness: 8
        - MinorCivWarBias: 5
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 5
        - Rationale: Reduce baseline aggression to enable strategic peacing out of the catastrophically draining war with The Huns, which has reached 84% war weariness and is crippling our empire's happiness and combat effectiveness. Lowering meanness and hostility while raising forgiveness makes the AI willing to accept or offer peace when it serves the greater goal of focusing on spaceship construction. We remain highly competitive and bold, but no longer irrationally committed to endless wars that have no strategic payoff.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - TargetID: 6
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Remove absolute hostility modifiers toward The Huns. This distant, weak civilization generates 84% war weariness and contributes nothing to our strategic objectives; the conflict serves only to sap our happiness and combat strength. Resetting relations to neutral improves the likelihood of negotiating peace, freeing resources and reducing empire-wide war exhaustion while we focus on critical fronts and the space race.
  - tokens:

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Game State Overview:**
            - Turn 438, playing as Assyria (Player 2)
            - Era: Information
            - We are currently at war with Austria, The Inca, The Huns, and Indonesia
            - Current Grand Strategy: Conquest
            - Score: 3197 (3rd place behind Austria 4509 and The Inca 3774)
            - Military Strength: 10509 (significantly weaker than Austria's 60117 and The Inca's 26694)
            
            **Victory Conditions:**
            - Domination: Need 8 capitals, control 2 (Assur, Addis Ababa) - 25%. Austria and The Inca also at 25%.
            - Science: Apollo program complete, 0 parts built. Some competitors also have Apollo.
            - Cultural: Influential over 1 civ (The Huns), Poland is almost there (5/6 influential). Austria has 4/6, Poland 5/6. This is not leading.
            - Diplomatic: Voting in 4 turns. Austria has 45 delegates (112%), we have 4 (10%). Global Hegemony proposal is active. Diplomatic victory seems out of reach.
            
            **Military Situation:**
            - War with Austria: Our war weariness 50%, theirs 68%. Our score 8%. Not great.
            - War with The Inca: Our war weariness 0%, theirs 1%. Our score 0%. Stalemate mostly.
            - War with The Huns: Our war weariness 84%, theirs 3%. Our score 12%. Very bad weariness.
            - War with Indonesia: Our war weariness 0%, theirs 2%. Our score 0%.
            
            The military strength disparity is concerning. Austria has 60k strength, The Inca 26k, we only have 10.5k. We're fighting on multiple fronts.
            
            **Strategic Challenge:**
            We have very high war weariness with The Huns (84%) which is crippling. We're also at war with Austria (50% weariness) and The Inca simultaneously. The Huns are actually a vassal of Austria, and Indonesia is a vassal of The Inca.
            
            Given our very aggressive persona and conquest strategy, we've been fighting constantly. But our military strength is now quite weak relative to opponents.
            
            **Current Flavors:**
            - Offense: 20, Defense: 80, Mobilization: 100, CityDefense: 30, MilitaryTraining: 90
            - Ranged: 95, Mobile: 20, Nuke: 100, UseNuke: 95
            - Naval: 90, Air: 100, AirCarrier: 80, Antiair: 60, Airlift: 100
            - Production: 100
            - Science: 50
            
            **Policy & Tech:**
            - Next Policy: Iron Curtain (Order Level 3) - good for conquest as it gives free courthouses and boosts internal trade routes
            - Next Tech: Robotics - important for SS Factory and Missile Cruiser, but also unlocks spaceship techs. Given we're building SS Boosters in some cities, this suggests a pivot toward science might be possible, but our Grand Strategy is Conquest.
            
            **Analysis of Options:**
            We need to seriously consider our position. We have 4 war weariness with Austria at 50% and The Huns at 84%. High war weariness means unhappiness and combat penalties. Our happiness is already "Unhappy - combat penalty" at 49%.
            
            However, looking at the Inca war - we seem to be making some progress. The military report shows "Steamroll" posture in zones near Arequipa, Vitcos, and Sydney. We're killing Incan infantry and taking territory.
            
            But we lost a Heavy Bomber to Austria (combat result 140) and another to The Inca (143, 146). We also lost a Jet Fighter.
            
            **Diplomatic Considerations:**
            - Poland is friendly (100/100), also Order ideology
            - Morocco is friendly (100/95), but Autocracy
            - Ethiopia is our friend but was defeated by The Inca
            - We have DENOUNCED Austria, The Inca, The Huns, Indonesia
            
            **Key Decision Points:**
            1. Should we continue conquest? Our military is weak but we have advanced tech (75 techs, leading).
            2. Should we switch grand strategy? We have Apollo built and are building SS Boosters - a science victory might be more achievable than domination given our relative weakness.
            3. The Huns war has 84% weariness - we should probably make peace if possible. But our persona has Forgiveness: 1 and Peace acceptance is affected by relationship (-100). With our WarBias 6, the AI might not seek peace.
            
            Wait, looking at the war with The Huns: "Our Score: 12%; Our War Weariness: 84%". The Huns have 3% weariness. This is terrible for us. We need to get out of this war.
            
            Similarly with Austria: "Our Score: 8%; Our War Weariness: 50%; Their War Weariness: 68%". Austria is actually more weary than us. But they have 60k military strength vs our 10k.
            
            Actually, let me reconsider the military numbers. Those might not be directly comparable or might include defensive structures. But still, 10k vs 60k is a big gap.
            
            **What can we do?**
            We could:
            1. Keep Conquest but adjust flavors to be more defensive while we rebuild
            2. Switch to Spaceship grand strategy and focus on science victory
            3. Try to peace out of some wars
            
            Important: The Huns vassalage - The Huns are Austria's vassal. Indonesia is The Inca's vassal. Vassals can't win independently but their master can.
            
            **Proposal: Grand Strategy Reassessment**
            Given that:
            - We have Apollo complete and are building SS Boosters
            - Our military is relatively weak (10.5k vs Austria 60k, Inca 26k, Poland 25k)
            - We're fighting 4 wars simultaneously with high weariness
            - Diplomatic victory is impossible (Austria has 45 delegates)
            - Cultural victory is unlikely (Poland leads with 5/6 influential)
            
            A science victory or time victory might be best. But our cities are building SS Boosters already (Nimrud, Kar-Tukulti-Ninurta, Gondar). This suggests the in-game AI has already been pivoting toward science under our conquest strategy? Or these are just what cities build when there's nothing else.
            
            Actually, looking at city production:
            - Assur: Strategic Defense System
            - Nineveh: Fighter
            - Nimrud: SS Booster
            - Kar-Tukulti-Ninurta: SS Booster
            - Halab: Seaport
            - Addis Ababa: Public Works
            - Harar: Seaport
            - Ica: Public Works
            - Sokoto: Military Academy
            - Dur-Sharrukin: Public Works
            - Gondar: SS Booster
            - Brussels: Public Works
            - Lalibela: Stadium
            - Axum: Public Works
            - Nippur: Forge
            - Argos: Military Base
            - Dire Dawa: Public Works
            
            SS Boosters are space ship parts. So we ARE building spaceship parts. But under Conquest grand strategy.
            
            Given the extreme war weariness with The Huns (84%) and the disadvantageous war with Austria, I think we should:
            1. Consider making peace where possible
            2. EITHER double down on conquest against The Inca (who we seem to be pushing) while peacing out others
            3. OR pivot to Spaceship grand strategy
            
            But looking at our relationships: we have set Public/Private opinions for The Huns at -100. The game might not let us peace out easily or the AI won't want to. With WorkAgainstWillingness at 10 and Loyalty at 1, making peace might actually be possible if it benefits us.
            
            Actually, I should look at whether peacing out is even strategically desirable. The Huns are far away and weak (score 1396, military 2509). They can't really threaten us. The war is just generating weariness. Same with Indonesia - they are weak and a vassal.
            
            The real threats/competitors are Austria (4509 score) and The Inca (3774 score). Poland is friendly with 3376 score.
            
            If we want to win, we probably need to:
            - Secure our position
            - Either eliminate The Inca (25% domination) or Austria (25% domination) OR go for science
            
            Given our unique ability (gain tech when conquering cities), conquest synergizes well with science. Also, our cities are already building spaceship parts.
            
            But with Austria having 60k military strength and 15 cities, conquering them seems very hard. The Inca has 20 cities and 26k military - also hard but we seem to be pushing them back.
            
            Wait, actually looking more carefully at the tactical zones:
            - Zone 8814 (Arequipa): Enemy zone, Dominance: Friendly, Posture: Steamroll, FriendlyStrength 5162 vs Enemy 769. We're crushing them.
            - Zone 12172 (Vitcos): Enemy zone, Dominance: Friendly, Posture: Steamroll, Friendly 4863 vs Enemy 764. Crushing.
            - Zone 20646 (Sydney): Even, Posture: Steamroll, Friendly 1307 vs Enemy 809. Winning.
            - Zone 10815 (Mataram/Indonesia): Even, Posture: Steamroll, Friendly 1213 vs Enemy 1063. Slight edge.
            
            So against The Inca and Indonesia, we're actually winning tactically! Against Austria, we're not seeing direct assault zones - the Austrian zones show "Withdraw" or no units near our core cities.
            
            Perhaps the military strength numbers are misleading because Austria's is concentrated elsewhere (fighting The Inca too).
            
            Actually Austria is at war with The Inca (score -2% for Austria, 26% weariness) and The Huns (vassal). So Austria is fighting on multiple fronts too.
            
            **Decision Framework:**
            1. We are building spaceship parts already.
            2. We are winning against The Inca locally.
            3. We have terrible war weariness against The Huns (84%).
            4. Austria is incredibly strong (60k) but also fighting The Inca.
            
            I think the best play is to:
            - Keep pushing against The Inca to try to capture Cusco or Jakarta (capitals) - this advances domination AND gives us techs via our UA.
            - Get out of the Hunnic war if possible (useless war, massive weariness).
            - Maintain defense against Austria.
            - Continue building spaceship parts as a backup.
            
            However, can I peace out? The relationship tool can set modifiers but doesn't directly force peace. Peace is a diplomatic action by the in-game AI. With our relationship at -100/-100 with The Huns and high war weariness, peace might actually be acceptable to both sides. But our persona has WarBias 6 (moderately warlike), HostileBias 10, Meanness 10 - the AI might refuse peace.
            
            I should probably lower hostile bias or adjust relationships to make peace more likely. But actually, the relationship values ADD to the AI's evaluation. Setting The Huns relationship to 0 (from -100) would help peace. But we're capped by our own opinions - we set public at -100.
            
            Wait, we can change our own relationship values. If we set public/private to something less negative, the AI's peace willingness might increase. But with our persona (Meanness 10, Forgiveness 1), the in-game AI will be very reluctant to make peace.
            
            Let me think about what to call:
            1. `set-flavors`: Keep conquest but adjust for the current situation. We need to push hard where we're winning (Inca), hold elsewhere. Actually, maybe we should increase city defense and mobile offense.
            2. `set-research`: Robotics is next. This is fine - it unlocks SS parts building bonus and Missile Cruiser.
            3. `set-policy`: Iron Curtain is next. Good for conquest (free courthouses on annex).
            4. `set-relationship`: Maybe adjust The Huns relationship to be less hostile? Or keep it? Given 84% weariness, we really should peace out. But our strategy has been maximum hostility. If we want peace, we need:
               - Reduce negative relationship modifiers
               - Maybe persona adjustments to be less warlike
            
            Actually, should we just commit fully to conquest? We're Ashurbanipal, our UA gives techs on city capture. We have Order ideology with Iron Curtain coming. If we can capture Incan cities, we get stronger.
            
            But we also have significant unhappiness (49%, Unhappy - combat penalty).
            
            Let me look at happiness. Our cities show:
            - Axum: -9
            - Dire Dawa: -11
            - Lalibela: -3
            - Brussels: -3
            - Nippur: -8
            - Others positive or small negative.
            
            Total population 406 over 17 cities. Average size 24. We have many occupied cities which probably lack courthouses.
            
            Iron Curtain gives free courthouses on annexation! That's huge for happiness.
            
            If we switch to Spaceship grand strategy, the AI will prioritize science, food, production, and slightly encourage settlement. But we're at war and building SS boosters already.
            
            If we keep Conquest, we prioritize production, food, gold, and settlement.
            
            Given that we are currently winning on the Inca front AND building spaceship parts, perhaps a hybrid approach: keep conquest but with scientific backup.
            
            Actually, I think the critical issue is war weariness with The Huns. 84% is devastating. Even if we don't want to be "friendly," we need to peace out. But our current persona makes peace nearly impossible.
            
            Wait - looking at the Huns relationship in the Diplomacy section: The Huns leader opinion of us is 75 (public) despite us being at war. That's because they "ignore most warmongering." Our opinion is -100. Their war weariness is only 3%. They might actually accept peace if we offered it, but our AI with Meanness 10 and Forgiveness 1 might not offer.
            
            I should adjust our persona to be slightly more willing to accept peace (lower Meanness, increase Forgiveness) and adjust the Hunnic relationship upward. But I need to be careful - the persona affects ALL diplomatic relations.
            
            Alternatively, I could just keep the status quo persona and trust that 84% war weariness will eventually force peace negotiations. But that's risky.
            
            Let me reconsider whether peace with The Huns matters. They are far away and weak. The weariness affects happiness and combat effectiveness empire-wide. Yes, it matters a lot.
            
            What about Indonesia? 0% weariness, they're even weaker. Peace with them doesn't matter much.
            
            What about Austria? 50% weariness, but they have huge army. We might want peace there too, but we're actually winning defensively (our score is positive 8%, theirs -8% in the war overview? Wait "Our Score: 8%; Our War Weariness: 50%" vs "Their War Weariness: 68%". Austria is actually more worn out! But their military is massive.
            
            I think we should:
            1. **Switch Grand Strategy to Spaceship** - we're building SS parts already, our military is too weak for pure domination against Austria, and this gives a clear win condition. The description says "Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement." This aligns with building SS boosters and recovering.
            2. **Keep strong military flavors but pivot emphasis** - we need to defend while we tech. Actually if we switch to Spaceship, we still need to win wars or peace out.
            3. **Keep Iron Curtain** - free courthouses help happiness regardless.
            4. **Keep Robotics** - essential for either path.
            
            But wait - is Grand Strategy: Spaceship actually available? Yes, it's in the options.
            
            If I switch to Spaceship:
            - +Science, food, production priority
            - Might discourage unnecessary warfare
            - Supports our current SS booster construction
            
            But we also need to deal with The Huns' war weariness. If I switch to Spaceship and increase peace willingness, the AI might peace out.
            
            Actually, there's another consideration: We're playing on Vox Populi. In VP, domination is often more viable than vanilla, but science is reliable. However, we're already in the Information Era with 75 techs. We have a tech lead (The Inca 73, Austria 72, Poland 70). Our science per turn is 3342.
            
            Let me look at what others are building. Are any other civs building SS parts? The victory progress says 0 parts completed for everyone. We have Apollo complete. That's a significant advantage.
            
            Given:
            - Tech lead (75 vs 73/72)
            - Apollo complete
            - Already building boosters
            - Weak relative military
            - Fighting multiple wars
            - Diplomatic victory impossible (Austria has 45 delegates)
            - Domination would require taking capitals from Austria or Inca who are very strong
            
            **Science victory is the most realistic path.**
            
            But we have to survive the wars. Switching to Spaceship grand strategy might not stop the AI from keeping us in wars. We need to either:
            A) Win the wars defensively and then launch spaceship
            B) Peace out of wars and focus on spaceship
            
            Given our persona is extremely hostile, peace might be hard. But if we're pursuing science, we should reduce unnecessary wars.
            
            Actually, if we capture Incan cities, we get technologies via our UA. So fighting The Inca actually helps science victory! If we capture Cusco or Jakarta (both capitals), we get a tech and advance domination. We seem to be pushing them successfully in Arequipa/Vitcos/Sydney.
            
            But The Huns and Austria wars don't help. Austria is too strong to conquer easily.
            
            **Plan:**
            1. Switch Grand Strategy to Spaceship to formalize the science focus
            2. Adjust flavors to support a "defense + science push" posture:
               - Keep Offense moderate but increase Defense
               - Keep Mobilization/Production for building parts
               - Increase Science
               - Keep Air/Naval for defense
               - Decrease Nuke/UseNuke (wasteful when focusing on spaceship, and we're not using them effectively)
            3. Adjust persona to be slightly less suicidally aggressive to allow peace with The Huns (reduce Meanness, increase Forgiveness slightly)
            4. Keep Research: Robotics
            5. Keep Policy: Iron Curtain (amazing for happiness from annexed cities)
            
            Wait, but our UA specifically rewards conquest. If we stop conquering, we lose that synergy. But we're not stopping entirely - just focusing on science.
            
            Actually, I think Conquest Grand Strategy might be fine IF we can direct it properly. The issue is that Conquest prioritizes production/food/gold and "slightly encourage settlement" while Spaceship prioritizes science/food/production. Given we're building SS Boosters, Spaceship gives better alignment.
            
            But will switching to Spaceship make us stop attacking The Inca? The flavors control that, not the grand strategy directly. Grand strategy affects overall weighting but flavors override tactical preferences.
            
            Let me think about flavors more carefully:
            - Offense: 20 (currently low)
            - Defense: 80
            - Mobilization: 100
            - CityDefense: 30 (low - we have many cities at risk?)
            - MilitaryTraining: 90
            - Recon: 10
            - Ranged: 95
            - Mobile: 20
            - Nuke: 100 / UseNuke: 95
            - Naval: 90
            - NavalRecon: 70
            - Air: 100
            - AirCarrier: 80
            - Antiair: 60
            - Airlift: 100
            - Production: 100
            - Science: 50
            
            This flavor set is extremely militarized. With Spaceship grand strategy, I'd want:
            - Science: 100 (we need to tech to spaceship parts)
            - Production: 100 (need hammers for parts)
            - Spaceship: 100 (prioritize parts)
            - Defense: 80+ (hold wars while we tech)
            - Offense: keep moderate against Inca
            - Growth: increase (more population = more science)
            
            But if we set everything to 100, nothing is prioritized. Need to be selective.
            
            Actually, looking at the cities again: Are we really going for science? We have SS Boosters building in Nimrud, Kar-Tukulti-Ninurta, and Gondar. That's 3 of 17 cities. Assur is building Strategic Defense System. Nineveh built a Fighter. These don't scream "pure science."
            
            But given our dire strategic position (multiple wars, 10.5k military vs 60k Austria), optimizing for the most likely victory path is crucial.
            
            **Final Analysis:**
            I think we should:
            1. **Switch to Spaceship Grand Strategy** - aligns with Apollo complete and SS boosters in production
            2. **Set flavors**: 
               - Spaceship: 100 (must finish those parts)
               - Science: 90 (tech to remaining spaceship techs)
               - Production: 90 (need production for parts)
